- **T3.7 feat(mining): drill re-equip fix [brief 3.8]** [dropped R37] **Removed at G3 (R37, 2026-10-15, your decision): the module, its mixin and hook `drill.refresh`, its card, tests and CHANGELOG entry are gone; the record below is kept.** It was built (done 2026-10-09) and removed in commit `792ec9c`; no other task depends on it. Was: (S, deps T3.0f, T1.9). A `@ModifyReturnValue` on `ItemInHandRenderer.shouldInstantlyReplaceVisibleItem` skips the hand dip only when all of these hold (D-4 (a)):
  - both stacks are the same SkyBlock drill: same item, same component types, same id, same non-empty uuid, drill-fuel data present
  - the player is on SkyBlock and the toggle is on

  It never forces the dip.
  - Was Req: REQ-DRILL-01, REQ-DRILL-02, REQ-DRILL-03, REQ-DRILL-04, REQ-DRILL-05, REQ-DRILL-06, REQ-DRILL-07, REQ-DRILL-08
  - Card "Drill re-equip fix" (Mining), ON. Its tooltip names the Skyblocker and NoFrills equivalents. No block-breaking, game-mode or packet code. (B) is excluded and no fuel HUD is built (D-4).
  - Was Accept: AC-DRILL-01, AC-DRILL-02 (review plus CI grep), AC-DRILL-05, AC-DRILL-06. AC-DRILL-04 (tier D) and AC-DRILL-03 are checked at G3.
  - Done (2026-10-09):
    - **Hook:** `mixin/DrillReequipMixin` (hook `drill.refresh`) is a `@ModifyReturnValue` on `ItemInHandRenderer.shouldInstantlyReplaceVisibleItem` (both hands go through it). It returns `original || sameDrill`, so it never forces the dip.
    - **Decision** (`drillfix/DrillRefresh`, a pure table) only reads the stacks when on SkyBlock with the toggle on. A refreshed copy must have:
      - the same item and component types;
      - the same `id` and the same non-empty `uuid`;
      - `drill_fuel` on both;
      - every other component value equal (only custom data, lore and damage may differ).
      The key names come from a real drill's custom data (Skyblocker's test dump). Whether a drill at 0 fuel keeps `drill_fuel` is UNVERIFIED; if not, its refresh simply dips as in vanilla.
    - **Card** "Drill re-equip fix" (Mining, ON): the tooltip names Skyblocker's "Cancel Component Update Animation" and NoFrills' "No Equip Animation", and says turning this one off does not turn theirs off. It shows unavailable if the hook was skipped. No fuel HUD, parser or warning (D-4).
    - **Tests:**
      - DrillRefreshTest: AC-DRILL-01's table, one-sided fuel, a renamed copy, stacks not read when gated, the card, and AC-DRILL-02/06 [R] as a source scan (no game mode, block breaking, packet, fuel parser or HUD; one hook only).
      - Gametest DrillFixGameTest [B], which reads the first-person hand height:
        - off SkyBlock, a fuel refresh dips (AC-DRILL-05);
        - forced SkyBlock: no dip;
        - another uuid: dip;
        - toggle off: dip.
    - **Open for G3:** AC-DRILL-03 (50 blocks in the Dwarven Mines with Skyblocker's and NoFrills' options off), and whether a drill at 0 fuel keeps `drill_fuel`; AC-DRILL-04 at the tier D boot.
    - **Review:** two angles with two verifiers each. Fixed (confirmed):
      - other component values were not compared (REQ-DRILL-01 allows only lore, custom data and damage to change);
      - one-sided fuel was untested;
      - the game used a different gate than the table tested;
      - the card was untested.
      Rejected (split): a Pickonimbus gametest step (the unit table covers it).

*T3.9 Rare Drop Odds [brief 3.7]: split into T3.9a–h and built last in Phase 3. D-3 = A (numeric odds, five cases), R10 = b. Reveal animations and the later candidates listed in SPEC rare-drop-odds are not in v1.* **Dropped by R42 (2026-10-15, your decision "Ignore the gambling overlays"): T3.9a, T3.9g and T3.9h were built and are removed from the code; T3.9b–f were never built. Nothing of it was released (it came after 1.2.0), so there is no config migration: a saved `rare_drop_odds` section is kept and not read. No active task depends on these tasks; the records below are kept.**

- **T3.9a feat(odds): odds math + "1 in N (P%)" format** [dropped R42] **Removed (R42, 2026-10-15, your decision): `OddsMath`, `OddsFormat` and OddsMathTest are gone; the record below is kept.** It was built (done 2026-10-09). Was: (S, deps T1.18). Pure Java; every value is computed in code: at least one success, expected attempts, luck percentile, MF/Pet Luck scaling (cap 900, the < 5 % rule), per-corpse chance from per-roll weights over uniform roll counts, RNG-meter boost, slayer base chance, and bosses until guaranteed.
  - Was Req: REQ-ODDS-06, REQ-ODDS-08
  - Format: thousands separators, 2 significant digits (minimum 0.0001 %), a basis label, "≈" for estimates, "?" for `verified: false` rows.
  - Was Accept: AC-ODDS-05, AC-ODDS-07.
  - Done (2026-10-09): `module/odds/`, plain Kotlin with no game types (the repo's language since R30).
    - **`OddsMath`:** at least one success, expected attempts, luck percentile, the pet and drop chance with MF capped at 900 (pets always scale, other drops only below 5 %), the per-corpse mean over uniform roll counts, the meter boost (at most ×3), "guaranteed", the slayer base (500, or 250 for the Riftstalker) and bosses until guaranteed (null before a gain is seen).
    - **`OddsFormat`:** "1 in N (P%) <basis>" with US thousands separators, and the six SPEC basis labels.
      - P has 2 significant digits, rounded half up on the decimal value (0.01 → "1.0%", 0.004 → "0.40%"), and shows "<0.0001%" below the minimum.
      - A chance of 1 or more is "1 in 1 (100%)"; 0 or NaN throws, since a missing number is the caller's "no number" case (REQ-ODDS-11).
      - A `verified: false` row ends in " ?"; the run estimate reads "≈ 1 in N runs (P%, base, no bonuses)".
    - **Tests:** OddsMathTest (7), every AC-ODDS-05 and AC-ODDS-07 fixture plus the boundaries.
    - **For T3.9b:** the Scatha "chance by now" at 0 kills is a computed 0. The panel must show it as such and not pass 0 to `oneIn`.
    - **Review:** two angles with two verifiers each. Fixed (confirmed): whole-number percentages lost their second digit ("1%"), and exactly 0.0001 % showed as below the minimum (both from rounding the binary double). Also taken: p ≥ 1 as 100 %, and the meter cap test.

The NEU-REPO notice keeps only the NPC list; SkyOcean's notice section stays only for the Bobber Fix approach credit. The validator keeps its per-row source rules (the corpse spot table uses `sourceRefs`) and drops the odds-only rules: no percentage field, no SkyOcean dye row, roll min at most max. The record below is kept.** It was built (done 2026-10-09). Was: (M, deps T3.0c, T3.0i, T3.0j). Three registry tables, each with a schema and an index entry:

Each row cites a source with `kind: wiki_crosscheck`, `access: user_supplied` (D-16 (a), D-28 (i)) and is `verified: false` unless a non-wiki origin agrees (REQ-ODDS-10). Rows are added only once you supply the pages; until then the table has no row for those items, so they show no number (REQ-ODDS-11). T3.9b and T3.9e add their rows; they create no table.
  - Was Req: REQ-ODDS-09, REQ-ODDS-10, REQ-XC-LICENSE-03
  - Each row holds the case id, SkyBlock id and display name; a weight plus the table total, or a base chance; roll min/max; `sourceRefs`, `license`, `verified` and `gameVersion`. Placeholder scores are flagged.
  - Was Accept: AC-ODDS-08 (all three tables; a wiki-only row with `verified: true` and no second non-wiki origin also fails): `check` rejects a row without `sourceRefs` or `license`, any per-corpse % field, SkyOcean's assumed dye weight, and an MIT table without a notice.
  - Done (2026-10-09):

    - **`odds.supplied`** (`odds/supplied.json`, CC0): empty. Rows come with the pages you supply (D-16 (a)): the Scatha pet chances, the Lapis/Umber/Tungsten per-roll chances, and the Frostbitten Dye per-roll chance (case `vanguard`, replacing SkyOcean's assumed weight).

The "Rare Drop Odds" card in Odds & Trackers: master toggle OFF; five case toggles ON; chat-line toggle ON; rare threshold 2 % (range 0.01–10 %). "Gambling" appears only as a hidden search keyword.
  - Was Req: REQ-ODDS-01, REQ-ODDS-02, REQ-ODDS-03, REQ-ODDS-04, REQ-ODDS-07, REQ-ODDS-11, REQ-ODDS-12
  - Inputs are read-only, through game-state, and each event gets at most one local line. An unknown item or floor shows no number and is logged once. A missing table disables only its own case and shows a card notice.
  - Per-profile counters use the T3.0d store and are labelled "counted by K8bas since <date>". A "Reset odds counters" button resets the session counters.

    - **`OddsTables`:** the three table specs, decoded to typed rows, registered before the registry starts. A test hook can treat a table as unreadable.
    - **`OddsCounters`** (per profile through ProfileService):

    - **`RareDropOddsModule`:** the Odds & Trackers card.
      - Master OFF; five case toggles, "Chat lines" and "Rare threshold" (a choice of 0.01 % to 10 %, default 2 %); "Reset odds counters".
      - "gambling" is only a keyword (P7).
      - The cases get `active(case)`, `post(case, line)` (one local line, when the chat lines are on), `unknown(case, name)`, `counters()` and `rareThreshold()`.
    - **`ProfileService.storable()`** says whether the current profile's state can be read and written.
    - **Tests:**
      - OddsFrameworkTest (13): the gate and AC-ODDS-09's table and log rules, the card (AC-ODDS-01), the counters (AC-ODDS-10, plus an unsettled profile and session resets), and the AC-ODDS-02 [R] source scan.

    - **Gametest hygiene:** ConfigScreenGameTest, ConfigMotionGameTest and KeyboardNavGameTest switched every module ON after themselves. With a feature that is OFF by default this kept the profile service reading for later gametests, so they now put each module back (`gametest/ModuleStates`).
    - **Review:** two angles with two verifiers each. Fixed (confirmed): counts made while the profile was unknown or unsettled were shown as zeros and lost; the session reset on a reconnect, on the id arriving or on a toggle; AC-ODDS-09's real table state and the once-per-session log were untested; the notice's case names were cut off. - [x] **T3.3a feat(data): corpse spot table [brief 3.3]** [reinstated R40] (M, deps T3.0c, T3.0i, T3.0j). Dropped by R22 and reinstated by R40. A registry table of possible corpse spots per shaft code (D-5, R40), under `data/thirdparty/skyhanni-repo/` (MIT), merged from three sources:
  - SkyHanni-REPO PR #759 (still open and unmerged) @ `e2c8edb93240b0852d9b42e614f3c80a5476916c`;
  - meowdding-repo `repo/mining/mineshaft_corpses/`, data unchanged since `42d01278a8`, pinned at `20524eb3255da52653957dfd39b22a02e56b82ad` (its MIT LICENSE is present there);
  - ShaftUtils (DeveloperGr3y/ShaftUtils, CC0-1.0) `corpse_spawns.json` @ `c9a38786f61533e63d9fef20f320c70b02072c4f` (105 spots).

  One frame, the block above the corpse armor stand (Skyblocker's "Found a … Corpse at"): PR #759 as it is, meowdding and ShaftUtils with y+1. Spots of one code within 2.0 blocks are one spot, in its most common variant; on a tie PR #759 wins over meowdding, and meowdding over ShaftUtils. Result: 29 codes and 129 spots. - [x] **T3.3e feat(mining): rule out spots by ding silence** (S, deps T3.3d). R44 (your request): with the Organ Donor ding, the possible corpse spots that cannot hold an unlooted corpse are removed, by silence only. Once a ding (the `DingHint` ding: harp, block category, within 4 blocks, in a shaft) has been heard in the current shaft visit, a spot is ruled out when the player spent 4 s within 16 blocks of it (3-D, to the spot's block centre, at every tick of that window, sampled back to back) without any ding. A ding starts the window over, and so does a pause of more than 250 ms in the samples. For the rule-out a ding counts within 8 blocks of the player (the HUD keeps 4).
  - Req: REQ-CORPSE-04, REQ-CORPSE-06, REQ-CORPSE-12
  - Why 16 blocks and 4 s: the ding fires for an unlooted corpse within 20 blocks, and dings repeat every 0.55–1.0 s in range; both are safety margins, and 4 s leaves room for a lag spike (review, 3 s at first).
  - A ding never rules out a spot, also not a far one (there can be several corpses). The pitch is not used to rule out. The sound's position is never used, and no entity is read.
  - Ruled-out spots stay removed for the shaft visit. All come back when the Frozen Corpses widget lists more corpses than before in the visit (a Dead Man's Chest) and on entering another shaft.
  - Own toggle "Remove ruled-out spots" on the corpse spots card, default ON, effective only with "Organ Donor ding hint" ON. `/ksu corpses` still lists every spot and marks the ruled-out ones "· ruled out".
  - This is the borderline behaviour of SPEC REQ-XC-RULES-06, approved by your explicit request (R44). FixedAnchorSourceTest and the send scan must still pass for the module (AC-CORPSE-04 [R] stays true).
  - Accept: AC-CORPSE-13, AC-CORPSE-14. AC-CORPSE-15 [E] is checked at the next field check.
  - Field check (2026-10-15): AC-CORPSE-15 passed with the jar at `e3a19b3`.

Toggle "Remove ruled-out spots" (`corpse_spots.rule_out`, ON) on the card before "Edit position". Tests: SpotRuleOutTest (7), DingHintTest, CorpseSpotRulesTest, OptionCatalogTest (default and search "ruled out"); CorpseSpotsGameTest AC-CORPSE-14 (removal, the list mark, both toggles OFF and ON again, a third corpse brings it back). Full gametest suite green.

### Phase 3u — UI review, released as 1.3.0 [decided R45, R46]

*Spec approved (R46, 2026-10-15; T3.14).* Your request from 2026-10-15, marked "for later" until R45: a review of the settings UI with improvements: a sharper, higher-resolution look; sliders that sometimes clip; and an overall smoother, nicer experience. It starts after v1.2.1 (Checkpoint G3r), comes before Phase 4, and ships as 1.3.0. R46 records your answers to the review proposal, and SPEC `ui-config` now holds the UI review as REQ-UI-27 to REQ-UI-47 with AC-UI-27 to AC-UI-50. Nothing in `src/` is committed before you approve that spec and the tasks below (T3.14).

**Spec:** SPEC `ui-config` REQ-UI-27–REQ-UI-47 (and the R46 amendments of REQ-UI-01, -02, -06, -17, -18, -19, REQ-HUD-07 and REQ-HUD-14), release REQ-REL-01, -04, -06 and -14; decision R46; defaults in §12.H (Smooth corners ON, Animations ON).
- **What is wrong today** (the review's findings): 26.2 keeps two GUI elements in their submission order only when their whole-GUI-pixel bounds overlap. The fill-based corners submit each physical-pixel row as its own element with bounds up to 1 GUI px too small, so a slider's accent fill is layered over a knob row at GUI scales 2–4 (the slim line you saw), and fills cover outline corners at scales 3 and 4. A dimmed knob also lets the fill show through. At scale 2 everything else looks soft because Smooth corners is off by default, lines, chevrons and the × are 2×2 blocks, the colour wheel is stretched by a non-integer factor, and motion moves in 2 physical px steps.
- **Order [decided R46]:** A the layering fix → B sharpness (Smooth corners default, discs and pills, sampling, the wheel, icons, hairlines) → C motion (the Animations switch first, then sub-pixel motion, smooth scrolling with the scrollbar and the wheel over sliders scrolling the page, the overlay transitions) → D corners and shadows (with the aligned slider column) → E feedback (cursors, tooltips, list hover, slider feel) → F screenshot matrix, Checkpoint G3u, then the 1.3.0 release. A comes first because every later sub-pixel position (B, C) would bring the layering bug back without it.
- **Tests:** each task adds unit tests where the logic is testable (Prove-It for the bug fixes); screenshot checks run at GUI scales 1–4 with the test clock. The task that changes pixels updates the references of the existing screenshot gametests (SmoothCornersGameTest, RenderKitGameTest, WidgetsGameTest, AdvancedWidgetsGameTest, ListTooltipGameTest, ConfigScreenGameTest, NoticeGameTest, HudEditorGameTest) and says why in its commit body.
- **Sources and clean room:** 26.2 API names (the GUI element interface, the texture setup, the sampler cache, the cursor API) are checked with `javap` on the 26.2 jars before use (source-driven-development). No decompiled Minecraft code and no code of another mod is copied (REQ-UI-24, REQ-XC-LICENSE-02); the review's layering simulation stays out of the repo. New files follow `docs/language-classification.md` (REQ-UI-26).
- **Size:** 23 tasks before G3u (16 M, 7 S; T3.15–T3.37) and 3 release tasks (S; T3.38–T3.40); about 12–16 working days before G3u, plus your field check (about 30 minutes) and the release. A pure `SmoothScroll` (whole-pixel target, shown value with exponential easing of about 50 ms from the UI clock, never past the target, snap within half a physical px, clamp) shared by `ScrollArea`, `VirtualList` and `DropdownModel`. The layout runs at the rounded shown offset and the fraction is drawn as a translation on the physical grid; wheel notches add to the target; thumb drags and track presses set both; bringing a control into view glides; a drag that starts mid-glide stops the glide.   - Done (2026-10-16): the new pure `SmoothScroll` keeps a whole-px target and works the shown offset out in closed form from where it was shown when the target last changed and the time since (exponential, τ = 50 ms, at the target from 400 ms on, never past it, clamped when the content shrinks; a target that does not change does not restart it; a GUI scale change through `setScale` puts it on the target, EC-UI-18; with motion off it is the target). `ScrollArea` and `VirtualList` keep `VirtualRows` as the whole-px layout of this moment (synced to the rounded shown offset when read and drawn, not in the input handlers, so a press meets what was drawn); the wheel and bringing a control into view glide, a thumb drag and a press on the track jump, the wheel during a thumb drag is ignored, and a press during a glide stops it where the content was last laid out (EC-UI-25; in the settings screen through `ConfigScreen.mouseClicked`, which also ends a thumb drag whose release went elsewhere). `DropdownModel` glides in whole rows (the arrow keys and the wheel; opening jumps) and `Dropdown` draws its rows clipped to the box at the glide, the outline over them. The fraction under 1 GUI px is drawn as a translation on the physical-pixel grid: `VirtualList` and the dropdown through their `drawShift`, the settings page through `ConfigScreen.pageShift` with the slide's (T3.25). `WidgetScreen` reads the UI time once per frame, so a frame's layout and drawing see one moment of every glide and animation. The sidebar's selected tab glides into view (`VirtualList.ensureVisible`), the database picker's new search jumps to the top (`setScroll(0)`), and the scroll area gets the GUI scale in `layout` before the scroll is read. While an overlay is open a reveal jumps, so a list opened with Enter on a half-hidden dropdown stays anchored, and an overlay opening stops a glide where the page was drawn. VirtualListTest and DropdownAndKeybindTest run on a motion-off source, their assertions unchanged. Checked with javap: Fabric's `TestInput.scroll(double)` (gametest API 5.1.0); no new Minecraft API.

It joins the corner and disc masks in their cache (`MaskKey.shadow`, baked by `CornerTextures`, so within the 64-mask bound, freed and rebaked on a GUI scale change, EC-UI-18; three keys per scale: the 8, 6 and 4 GUI px corners), rather than a separate `ShadowTextures`. `ShapePlan.shadow` makes one textured element of up to eight slices: the corner tiles and the edges stretched from the texel next to the mask's centre, sampled at its centre, no centre piece; its bounds rounded outward and cut to the scissor or the window. `Shapes.shadow` draws it with the masks' linear, clamp-to-edge sampler, tinted black at `SHADOW_ALPHA` 0x50, `SHADOW_OFFSET` 1 GUI px down and `SHADOW_BLUR` 3 out, so its reach fits in the 4 GUI px a panel keeps from the window (REQ-UI-02, EC-UI-19) and it is never cut at the window's edge; it is left out when its mask cannot be had. ### Phase 4 — Auto-updater on GitHub Releases

*T1.4 already ships the release source and selection as v1.1.0's notify-only check (REQ-UPD-01, R4). Phase 4 completes that check and adds the confirmed, staged install (D-14). No Hypixel play is needed in this phase. It starts after the UI review (Phase 3u, released as 1.3.0) [decided R45]. It ships as v1.3.1, and its commits stay on this machine, one per task, until Checkpoint G4's push [decided R48].*

  - Hash availability (T4.2) and install eligibility (T4.3) plug in as hooks. Until those tasks land, the hooks answer notify-only.
  - Manual checks have a 60 s cooldown and respect rate-limit waits. Only one check or download runs at a time (the lock is shared with T4.2).
  - The state file on the REQ-CFG-12 facility gains `skippedVersion`, the pending-install record and the warned file names.
  - Accept: AC-UPD-04 manual rows (cooldown message, one check in flight); the manual-check row of AC-UPD-09; AC-UPD-07 second bullet (the manual "no build" text); AC-UPD-10 [A] manual part ("Couldn't reach GitHub" within 25 s); the state-file part of AC-UPD-20; the T1.4a/T1.4b tests re-run unchanged. Tests use recorded API JSON and a local mock server.
  - Done (2026-10-17): `UpdateService.checkManual()` returns the new sealed `CheckOutcome` (Kotlin: `Found` with the hook verdicts, the skipped and beta marks and `installOffered`; `NoBuild` with the release page; `UpToDate`; `NotARelease`; `RateLimited`; `Unreachable`; `SourceUnavailable`; `Cooldown`; `Busy`), each with the REQ-UPD-08 texts, times as HH:mm in the zone the caller passes. It runs at most once per 60 s (`CheckPolicy.manualAllowedAt`; a last manual check in the future does not hold one back), sends nothing inside a rate-limit wait (`manualRateLimited`; `CheckPolicy.apply` now records the wait's kind, and a wait saved before T4.1 counts as a rate limit), is not held back by the 6 h interval or a 5xx or 404 back-off, does not count against the automatic cap, and works with the toggle OFF. One `UpdateLock` is held by an automatic or a manual check (T4.2's download takes it too; a thread that fails to start releases it). `UpdateHooks` carries the hash check (by default GitHub's digest, only "sha256:" with 64 hex digits, lowercased) and the install eligibility (by default notify-only: "Install it by hand from the release page"); T4.2 and T4.3 replace them. `UpdateState` gains `lastManualMs`, `waitKind`, `skippedVersion` (compared as a version), `pendingInstall` (the new record `PendingInstall`) and `warnedFiles`; they are optional, so an older file loads with them empty and `stateVersion` stays 0 (the store's comment now says when a step is needed). No caller yet: `/ksu update` and the notices are T4.4, the card T4.4b; no player-visible change, so no CHANGELOG entry. No Minecraft API.

Unexpected type", "expected: <Missing> but was: <Verifiable[sha256=abc]>", "expected: <[v1.2.0 is available (skipped)]> but was: <[v1.2.0 is ...". - [x] **T4.2 feat(update): secure download + integrity + jar validation** (M, deps T4.1). Streams the confirmed asset to `.k8bas_update/*.part` (REQ-UPD-09 timeouts: connect 10 s, download 120 s), checks its SHA-256 and reads only its `fabric.mod.json`. It needs a consent token, which T4.4's confirmation issues.
  - Req: REQ-UPD-08, REQ-UPD-09, REQ-UPD-13, REQ-UPD-14, REQ-UPD-15, REQ-UPD-20
  - Hash source: the API `digest`, otherwise the `.sha256` sidecar. If neither exists, or the two disagree, the result goes to the notify-only or conflict row of REQ-UPD-08.
  - The selection → digest → jar-validator chain stays callable from tests for T7.4's draft check (AC-REL-09).
  - Accept: AC-UPD-14, AC-UPD-15, AC-UPD-16, the download-host part of AC-UPD-21; EC-UPD-03.
  - Done (2026-10-18): `UpdateService.download(Consent)` takes the token T4.4's confirmation issues (`Consent`: the version, and from the screen the SHA-256 it showed; `Consent.typed` for the command). It fetches no jar byte unless the version is the candidate on offer now (a skipped one included), else `DownloadOutcome.Stale` names the current one ("v2.0.0 is not the update on offer; that is v2.0.1"); a release file changed since the screen gives `Changed`; without a verifiable SHA-256 or an eligible game it is `NotOffered` with REQ-UPD-08's reason (`UpdateHooks.notOfferedReason`, shared with the check). It holds T4.1's `UpdateLock`, which now records whether a check or a download holds it, read in the same step as the take: "An update is already downloading", and a check meanwhile "An update is downloading, try again when it is done". `SecureDownloader` (Java): HTTPS on port 443 to github.com or a *.githubusercontent.com subdomain with no user part, asked on every hop; redirects followed by hand, at most 5 (a sixth ends it before its request); a final 200; `Content-Length` and the counted bytes equal to the asset's size, and over 32 MiB refused before any request; one 120 s deadline across hops with a watchdog that closes a stalled body; SHA-256 streamed; only User-Agent and Accept sent; only scheme and host logged. `JarStager` streams to `.k8bas_update/staged/<asset name>.part` (only names of the asset pattern), compares the SHA-256 (`MessageDigest.isEqual`), checks the jar and only then renames it to the asset name (`ATOMIC_MOVE`); every failure deletes the `.part`, and `cleanParts()` at launch removes one a closed game left (EC-UPD-03); `mods/` is never touched. `JarValidator` (Kotlin) reads only `fabric.mod.json`, at most 64 KiB whatever the zip declares: id, version equal to the asset's "<version>+<mc>" (`AssetSelector.declaredVersion`), environment client or * (absent reads as *), and `depends.minecraft` and `depends.fabricloader` through the Loader's own `VersionPredicate` (a string, or an array of which one must match; a missing one is rejected); a failure is "Incompatible build, nothing changed" with the reason logged. `ReleaseHash`: the digest, or the sidecar `<name>.sha256` (at most 1 KiB, one BOM, first token 64 hex, lowercased) when the digest is missing or not SHA-256; both present and different is `Conflict` with one WARN; a sidecar that exists but cannot be read now is the new `Hash.Unavailable` ("Couldn't read its checksum file from GitHub, try again later"), asked again at the next check; a read sidecar is remembered for the session. A check reads the sidecar before any confirmation, never the jar (R49); it gets the API's 20 s budget. `Updates` builds both downloaders with the GitHub rule (also under the development source override) and the staging folder `.k8bas_update` in the game directory; eligibility stays notify-only until T4.3, so the game downloads nothing yet: no player-visible change, no CHANGELOG entry. Checked with javap: Fabric Loader 0.19.5 `VersionPredicate.parse(String)` and `Version.parse(String)` (and probed: `"~"` and `"<>1"` fail to parse, `">=>"` parses); JDK 25's `HttpClient` default `followRedirects()` is NEVER (probed).
    Before: against stubs, 29 of the 32 first tests failed (e.g. "expected: <it needs Fabric Loader >=0.20.0, not 0.19.5> but was: <stub>", "expected: <Failed[reason=HTTP 404]> but was: <Failed[reason=stub]>", "Unexpected type, expected: <...DownloadOutcome.Staged>"); the other 3 only asserted a failure, and the review made them assert the reason. With the review's fixes reverted one at a time: "expected: <Unavailable> but was: <Missing>", "Unexpected type, expected: <...DownloadOutcome.Changed>", the sidecar size rule "expected: <Missing> but was: <Unavailable>", the redirect cap "expected: <Failed[reason=more than 5 redirects]> but was: <Failed[reason=HTTP 500]>", and without the read cap the zip-bomb test runs the test JVM out of heap. The `Content-Length` catch cannot be shown red: the JDK's HTTP/1.1 parser rejects a non-numeric value first, so only HTTP/2 could reach it. After: `SecureDownloaderTest` 9, `JarValidatorTest` 8, `ReleaseHashTest` 7 and `UpdateDownloadTest` 13 cases pass (AC-UPD-12 with a check that asks only for the sidecar; AC-UPD-14's cases, each by its reason and request count, with no `.part` and the whole game folder byte-identical; AC-UPD-15's six rows; AC-UPD-16; EC-UPD-03 with a connection dropped mid-body; the lock both ways; AC-REL-09's chain of selection, digest and sidecar, declared version and jar check on a local jar); T4.1's and T1.4's tests unchanged; 1144 unit tests; `UpdateNoticeGameTest` and `SettingsScreensGameTest`.
    - Review: correctness and security, and the tests against the ACs, each finding checked in the code or by a run; confirmed and fixed: a sidecar that could not be read let the digest alone allow an install (now `Unavailable`); the sidecar used the 120 s download budget inside a check (now 20 s); a confirmation named only a version, so a re-uploaded file could be fetched unseen (`Changed`); the lock's holder was read after a failed take; a malformed `Content-Length` could throw out of `fetch`; the mock never dropped a connection, so EC-UPD-03 ran as a 5 s timeout (a raw-socket server now cuts the body: 3 ms, "the body broke off"); the AC-UPD-14 cases asserted only a failure; the `mods/` snapshot could not change (now the whole game folder); the asset-name test never reached its branch (the unreachable containment branch is gone; the name pattern is the gate); the AC-REL-09 chain left out the sidecar and the declared version; the boundaries (exactly 32 MiB is asked for, a chunked body of exactly the size, a 1 KiB sidecar, a 64 KiB `fabric.mod.json`, a zip that declares less than it holds); the headers checked only by absence (now an allowlist) and the GitHub rule never through `fetch`; waits by timing (now a latch). Decided as R49: AC-UPD-12 against the sidecar read at the check. Not changed: the sidecar is remembered by name, URL and size, so a same-size re-upload of only the `.sha256` in one session is missed (the download still compares the bytes, so it fails safe); the automatic check does not run the hash check yet (T4.4 moves its notices onto `CheckOutcome`); the zip-bomb test takes about 2 s.
- [x] **T4.3 feat(update): install eligibility + staged install via helper** (M, deps T4.2). Decides whether an install may be offered, stages the verified jar outside `mods/`, and has a JDK-only helper swap it in after the game exits.
  - Req: REQ-UPD-16, REQ-UPD-17
  - Each REQ-UPD-16 reason (dev run, origin not a single `.jar`, unwritable folder, non-SemVer version, launcher-managed instance, no SHA-256) is shown by name.
  - Helper: puts the new jar in before taking the old one out, re-checks SHA-256, uses bounded waits and writes `helper.log`. Across drives it uses a temp name (EC-UPD-07).
  - Accept: AC-UPD-17 ([A] tables), AC-UPD-19; helper state-machine tests on a temp folder; EC-UPD-04, EC-UPD-06.
  - Done (2026-10-18): `InstallEligibility` (Kotlin, the hook T4.1 left notify-only) names each REQ-UPD-16 reason: a development run ("This is a development run, so nothing is installed here"); not exactly one regular `.jar` from a PATH origin (a folder, nested, unknown); the jar or its folder not writable (`Files.isWritable`, nothing written to probe it); a version that is not SemVer; a launcher-managed instance ("This instance is managed by your launcher, so update it there": a Prism/packwiz `mods/.index/*.pw.toml` whose `filename` names our jar, compared without case; a CurseForge `minecraftinstance.json`; a Modrinth App `profiles` folder, the last two unverified). No SHA-256 stays T4.2's row, which the hash check names before eligibility is asked. A confirmed, checked download is now armed: `StagedInstall` keeps the new jar in the running jar's folder under its asset name and never the running jar's own name, compared without case (AC-UPD-19), copies the helper's one class file out of the mod into `.k8bas_update/helper/`, records the `PendingInstall` (now with the version that staged it and the old jar) and registers one shutdown hook; a second confirmation replaces the first and deletes its staged jar. At exit the hook re-checks the staged jar's SHA-256, writes the `apply-attempt` marker for T4.3b and starts `javaw` from the game's own Java in `.k8bas_update` (class path `helper`, relative, so a ';' in the game path cannot split it), without waiting. `UpdateHelper` (Java, JDK only, no nested class) waits at most 120 s for the game's PID, checked against its start time so a reused PID is not waited for (EC-UPD-06: only its own game); checks the staged jar; moves it in atomically, or across drives copies it to `<name>.k8bas-part`, which Fabric ignores, flushes and renames it (EC-UPD-07); flushes the new jar to disk and checks it again; and only then deletes the old jar. Each move or delete is retried for at most 60 s; on a timeout the old jar stays. Another file under the new name is never overwritten. Every step goes to `.k8bas_update/logs/helper.log`. `DownloadOutcome.Staged` now reads "Update ready, restart Minecraft to apply". `UpdateService.cancelPending()` (REQ-UPD-12, for T4.4's button and command) disarms first, deletes the staged files and clears the record, takes the lock (`BUSY` beside a download) and is not a skip. `Updates` builds the eligibility from the Loader's `ModOrigin` (`getPaths()` only for kind PATH, checked with javap: `ModOrigin.getKind()`, `getPaths()`, `ModOrigin.Kind` PATH/NESTED/UNKNOWN; `FabricLoader.getGameDir()`, `isDevelopmentEnvironment()`). Nothing calls `download` yet (T4.4), so no player-visible change and no CHANGELOG entry.
    Before: against stubs, 21 of the first 25 tests failed (e.g. "expected: <Eligibility[eligible=false, reason=dev]> but was: <Eligibility[eligible=true, reason=null]>", "expected: <null> but was: <stub>", "expected: <[Update ready, restart Minecraft to apply]> but was: <[v2.0.1 is downloaded and checked]>"). With the review's fixes reverted one at a time: the copy path's staged delete "expected: <0> but was: <1>"; the jar's own writability "a read-only jar can be replaced but not deleted"; the start-time check "expected: <0> but was: <1>"; the absolute class path "expected: <[-Xmx32m, -cp, helper, ...]>"; the packwiz case; the first SHA-256 check "expected: <true> but was: <false>"; the delete retry "expected: <0> but was: <3>"; the origin kind "nested in another jar". After: `InstallEligibilityTest` 6 (with a real Windows ACL that denies writing and a read-only jar, through `Files.isWritable`), `StagedInstallTest` 7, `UpdateInstallTest` 6 and `UpdateHelperTest` 16 cases pass (nothing moves while the game lives; only its own game is waited for; a reused PID; a lock that ends within the retry time and one that does not; the other-drive name and a copy that keeps failing; a staged copy that cannot be deleted; a changed staged jar; a moved jar that fails its check; the 120 s and 60 s bounds; each refusal by its reason; the helper from its one class file in its own JVM). The tests that hold a file open run on Windows only, as Linux deletes open files. 1179 unit tests; `UpdateNoticeGameTest` and `SettingsScreensGameTest`.
    - Review: correctness and security, and the tests against the ACs, each finding checked in the code or by a run; no High or Medium. Confirmed and fixed: the other-drive copy was not flushed before the old jar went, nor the staged `.part` before its rename (a power cut could leave a jar of zeros alone); a staged copy that could not be deleted after the copy turned a done swap into "not applied"; a ';' in the game path split the class path; a read-only old jar could never be deleted; a refused second arm of the same version deleted the armed file; and as hardening, the start time against PID reuse, never deleting when the new and old jar are one file, cancel disarming before it clears the record, a directory error in the launcher check, the packwiz name without case, and the helper class loaded when arming rather than in the hook. In the tests: the locked-jar case would fail on CI's Linux; the retry, the first SHA-256 check, two refusal rules, the origin kind and the temporary name could be removed unnoticed; "waits for the game" was shown only by log order; EC-UPD-06, the bounds, a real read-only folder, a busy cancel and the exact files a download adds were untested; helper processes could outlive a failed test. Not changed: a Modrinth App data folder the player renamed is not recognised (unverified marker); Defender's Controlled Folder Access or a OneDrive lock is invisible to `Files.isWritable` (the helper then fails safely and T4.3b disarms after two tries); a helper killed mid-copy can leave `<name>.k8bas-part` in `mods/`, which T4.3b sweeps; flushing to disk and the same-version arm refusal are not shown by a test.
- [x] **T4.3b feat(update): launch reconciliation** (S, deps T4.3). Runs offline on every launch and finishes, re-arms, disarms or clears the staged update. Replaces the old "clean up stale copies / 1.0.x `deleteOnExit` leftovers" item (R13, R16).
  - Req: REQ-UPD-18
  - Deletes only the jar the updater replaced itself. Any other copy of our mod id is kept and warned about once (a WARN plus a local chat line; the file names are kept in the state file).
  - Two failed applies in a row disarm the update and point the user to a manual install.
  - Accept: unit tests for every REQ-UPD-18 branch; EC-UPD-05, EC-UPD-08, EC-UPD-15; runtime proof in AC-UPD-18 (T4.5).
  - Done (2026-10-18): `LaunchReconciler` (Kotlin) runs once per launch through `UpdateService.reconcileAtLaunch()`, on a virtual thread when the client has started, before the automatic check; it holds the lock (the new holder RECONCILE, which a check or download reads as busy) and waits up to 30 s for it rather than skip a launch; no network. Applied (the running version is the target): "Updated to vX" as a toast through the notice component, the record and the staged files cleared, the helper log kept, and the replaced jar deleted only when the helper ran (its `apply-attempt` marker); a jar left by a hand install is another copy (R13). A replaced jar still locked goes into `leftoverJar`: the helper deletes it at exit in its new `--delete-only` mode (`StagedInstall.deleteAtExit`), and every launch tries again. Not applied (the version that staged it still runs): the staged jar re-checked, eligibility asked again (REQ-UPD-16), re-armed with the jar that runs now, "Update ready, restart Minecraft to apply"; with the marker present the helper ran and failed, so it is counted (`PendingInstall.failedApplies`) and reported on the first join ("The update to vX couldn't be installed when you quit; it tries once more next time", EC-UPD-08); a hard kill counts nothing; two failures in a row disarm with "The update to vX could not be installed, so install it manually from the release page". Orphans (a record without its staged jar, staged files without a record), a record for neither version, a file name that is no jar of this mod and a failed re-check are cleared and logged. Other copies of the mod id in the running jar's folder are kept, named once in a WARN and a chat line on the first join (`UpdateNotifier.showOnJoin`), their names kept in `warnedFiles` (EC-UPD-15); the updater's own replaced jar is not one of them. A `<name>.k8bas-part` a killed helper left in `mods/` is swept. The state file is not trusted for paths: a file is deleted only if it is a jar of this mod beside the running one and not the running jar; the staged folder is never followed through a link or junction. Each step fails on its own, and the marker is consumed only after the state is saved. EC-UPD-05 needs nothing of its own: a crash exit runs the hook, so the next launch finds the target running with the marker. A development run has no running jar, so no mods folder is touched (seen in the gametest log). No player-visible change until T4.4 lets an update be confirmed, so no CHANGELOG entry.
    Before: against stubs, 11 of the first 12 tests failed (e.g. "expected: <Updated to v2.0.1> but was: <null>", "expected: <[Another copy of K8bas Skyblock Utility is in your mods folder: my-old-k8bas.jar]> but was: <[]>", the helper's "--delete-only" "expected: <0> but was: <2>"). With the review's fixes reverted one at a time, each red: the folder, mod-id and running-jar guards ("oldJar ...outside...", "...other.jar", "...2.0.1+26.2.jar"), the marker rule for a hand install, the replaced jar named as another copy ("the updater's own jar is not another copy"), the file-name check, the eligibility at re-arm, the re-arm's old jar ("the jar that runs now is the one to replace"), the first-failure line, the late marker delete, and waiting for the lock ("waiting for the download"). After: `LaunchReconcilerTest` 18 cases (one, the locked jar, on Windows only; the leftover path also on any system), `UpdateInstallTest` 8, `StagedInstallTest` 8 and `UpdateHelperTest` 17 pass; 1201 unit tests; `UpdateNoticeGameTest` and `SettingsScreensGameTest`.
    - Review: correctness and safety, and the tests against REQ-UPD-18, each finding checked in the code or by a run; no Critical or High. Confirmed and fixed: an `IOException` or a bad path stopped the launch check before the save (and the automatic check after it); the toast went only to the log; the first failed install was not reported (EC-UPD-08); the replaced jar waiting for its delete was named as another copy; a busy lock skipped the launch check; the marker was deleted before the save; the re-arm kept the old jar's path from staging and skipped the eligibility check; the staged folder could be followed through a junction; and, decided by R13's reading, the replaced jar is deleted only when the helper ran. In the tests: the path guards, the file-name branch, the refused re-arm, a stale marker, the state file round trip of the new fields, the lock wait and the leftover path on Linux were untested, and the locked-jar test passed for the wrong reason. Not changed: starting the game again while the last helper still waits (up to two minutes) can leave both jars and clear the record, never zero copies (a helper lock file would close it); the delete-only helper trusts its `--old` argument, which only the guarded game side writes; `showOnJoin` is shown in a gametest with T4.4's notices.

  - Delivered (2026-10-18):
    - **What changed:** the updater on GitHub Releases replaces 1.0.x's Modrinth updater: the check, its outcomes and its state file (T4.1); the download, its SHA-256 and the jar check (T4.2); the install eligibility and the swap by a helper process after the game exits (T4.3); the launch check (T4.3b); the toast, the first join's chat line, the confirmation screen and `/ksu update` (T4.4); the General › Updates card (T4.4b); the Windows run with a test build (T4.5); the G4 review fixes. Decisions R48–R55. `CHANGELOG.md` `[Unreleased]` has the player-visible changes.
    - **Tested:** see the tiers above and each task's Done note: [A] 1255 unit tests, [B]/[C] the full gametest suite, [D] the T4.5 run (AC-UPD-18 (a)–(e), a Prism-managed instance, a read-only mods folder, a dev run, an offline start) and the tier D boot.
    - **The 1.0.x updater, reviewed (REQ-UPD-02):** it checked Modrinth's version list once per launch and per *Check now* click, for Minecraft "26.1.2" written into the code and the `fabric` loader, with no state between checks; the project returns 404, so it never found anything. It compared versions with Fabric's `SemanticVersion`, falling back to a string compare. It downloaded the primary file with no status check, size cap, timeout or redirect handling, checked a SHA-1 taken from the same response, wrote the file straight into `mods/` under the name the server gave, and called `deleteOnExit` on the running jar, which Windows never honours. Checking and downloading were both on by default, with no confirmation, and its chat notices were dropped while no world was loaded. The study is `.claude/agent-memory/researcher/scratchpad-2026-10-16/research-updater.md` (local).
    - **U1–U20 (AC-UPD-02):**

      | Id | Severity | Finding | Disposition |
      |---|---|---|---|
      | U1 | High | `deleteOnExit` cannot delete the running jar on Windows | Fixed: REQ-UPD-17 (a helper swaps the jar after the game exits), REQ-UPD-18 (a leftover goes at the next launch); T4.5 (a), (c) |
      | U2 | High | no confirmation; check and download on by default | Fixed: REQ-UPD-11 (the screen's click is the only consent, R54), REQ-UPD-19 (the download flag removed), REQ-UPD-01 |
      | U3 | High | a non-atomic write straight into `mods/` | Fixed: REQ-UPD-13 (a `.part` file outside `mods/`), REQ-UPD-17 (moved in after the exit) |
      | U4 | High | the Minecraft version written into the code | Fixed: REQ-UPD-06 (the asset for the running version), REQ-UPD-15 (`depends.minecraft` checked), REQ-UPD-01 |
      | U5 | Medium | a server-given file name could leave `mods/` | Fixed: REQ-UPD-06 (an anchored asset name), REQ-UPD-17 and AC-UPD-19 (containment) |
      | U6 | Medium | a file name equal to the running jar's overwrote it | Fixed: REQ-UPD-17, AC-UPD-19 (refused) |
      | U7 | Medium | `getPaths()` throws for an origin that is not a path | Fixed: REQ-UPD-16 (the origin kind is checked; notify-only) |
      | U8 | Medium | no connect or request timeout | Fixed: REQ-UPD-09 (connect 10 s, request and download budgets) |
      | U9 | Medium | no single-flight guard | Fixed: REQ-UPD-04 (one lock for the check, the download, cancel and the launch check) |
      | U10 | Medium | notices dropped while no world is loaded | Fixed: REQ-UPD-10 (a toast on any screen, the line at the first join) |
      | U11 | Medium | redirects not followed, the status not checked | Fixed: REQ-UPD-13 (hops followed by hand to allowed hosts, a final 200) |
      | U12 | Low | integrity only, by a SHA-1 from the same response | Fixed: REQ-UPD-14 (SHA-256 from GitHub's digest and the sidecar), REQ-UPD-15 (the jar's own metadata) |
      | U13 | Low | bodies read without a limit | Fixed: REQ-UPD-09 (2 MiB for the list), REQ-UPD-13 (32 MiB, the size must match) |
      | U14 | Low | a string compare for versions it could not parse | Fixed: REQ-UPD-05 (strict SemVer; notify-only for a running version that is not) |
      | U15 | Low | a missing field ended the check; a `files[0]` fallback | Fixed: REQ-UPD-09 (a malformed list is one error), REQ-UPD-06 (a strict asset pattern, no fallback) |
      | U16 | Low | the scheme was not checked | Fixed: REQ-UPD-13, REQ-UPD-20 (https on every hop) |
      | U17 | Info | `mods/` assumed at `gameDir/mods` | Fixed: REQ-UPD-16, REQ-UPD-17 (the running jar's folder, checked for writing) |
      | U18 | Info | launcher-managed instances not detected | Fixed: REQ-UPD-16 (Prism and packwiz, CurseForge, Modrinth App are notify-only); T4.5 Prism run |
      | U19 | Info | zip slip | Not applicable: nothing is extracted; REQ-UPD-15 reads only `fabric.mod.json`, at most 64 KiB |
      | U20 | Info | threading | Accepted and kept: background threads and one lock; the render thread reads saved copies (G4) |

    - **Known issues:** v1.0.x installs keep their Modrinth updater, which cannot see GitHub releases, so their players update by hand once; the Corpse ding element sits under the HUD editor's toolbar; the opportunistic Phase 3 checks AC-MSA-10 and AC-GLOW-11 are still open. In T4.5 the consent was given by a script (the click itself rests on `UpdateNoticeGameTest`), and a failing DNS lookup of api.github.com was not run (AC-UPD-10 names a refused connection and a black-holed host; both were).

R53 (no notice for a staged version), R54 (the screen's click is the only consent) and R55 (a clicked skip confirm or cancel asks first) were recorded during the work without asking, as they only tighten what the spec allowed; say if you want any of them otherwise.

### Phase 5 — Bestiary Tracker HUD

bestiary-hud owns the family table (T5.1) and the Bestiary menu parser (T5.2b). Phase 6 reuses both and adds no second copy (REQ-BEST-10, REQ-BEST-15). Widget and menu fixtures stay UNVERIFIED until the G1 captures replace them (D-17, all captures at G1). Before T5.2 starts, the G1 widget capture decides between normal mode and the R11 fallback.

- [x] **T5.1 feat(bestiary): family table + tier math** (M, deps T3.0c). The trimmed NEU `bestiary.json` (pinned commit `24564a1ad2`, 359 families) becomes a registry table under `data/thirdparty/` with its MIT notice [decided D-5]. Tier math gives kills to the next tier, kills to max, and "MAX".
  - Req: REQ-BEST-15, REQ-BEST-03, REQ-BEST-04, REQ-XC-LICENSE-03
  - Row: slug id, display name, island/category, bracket or bracket-set id, cap, max tier count, mob ids. A cap outside the bracket list ends on the cap. CRITTERS families use their own set. Phase 6 references families as `bestiary:<id>`.
  - Accept: AC-BEST-02, AC-BEST-14 (bestiary rule in the `check` validator).
  - Done (2026-10-18):
    - **Table** `thirdparty/neu-repo/bestiary/families.json` (`bestiary`, MIT, 359 rows, 67 KB): NEU-REPO `constants/bestiary.json` at `24564a1ad22fdb0ca985d78710c401898eac586b` (the scratchpad copy is byte-identical to it; master has not changed the file since), built by a script outside the repo. Brackets `1`–`8` and the CRITTERS set as `critters_1`–`critters_5`; the 21 categories with their menu names, Fishing and Critter Safari with subcategories; per row id, name without colour codes, category, subcategory, bracket, cap, `maxTiers` and mob ids. Ids are the name slug; Bat and Enderman, the only names on two categories, get `_<category>` (`bat_dynamic`, `bat_catacombs`). A shipped id never changes; the rule is in the schema.
    - **Schema** `bestiary.schema.json`; **validator** (`DataValidator`): the pinned revision and its 359 families (moved together), every bracket increasing, each row's bracket, category and subcategory the table's own (a Fishing or Safari row needs its subcategory), `maxTiers` = the bracket's thresholds below the cap plus the cap; a value of the wrong type is left to the schema's message instead of ending the validation. THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES' NEU-REPO section names the table, the revision and how it was reshaped; `licenses/neu-repo.txt` was already shipped.
    - **Runtime** `BestiaryTable` (Kotlin, `module/bestiary`): the decoder, `BestiaryFamily.progress(kills)` → `TierProgress` (tier, max tier, next threshold, to next, to max, `isMax()`), `BestiaryTiers` (thresholds below the cap, then the cap; also for thresholds from the widget later), category names for the menu titles. A row whose `maxTiers` disagrees or whose bracket is unknown is skipped with one WARN; a repeated id keeps the first. Nothing registers the table yet (`/ksu debug data` lists it as "not used") until T5.2; no player-visible change, so no CHANGELOG line. No Minecraft or Fabric API used, so no javap check.
    - Before: against stubs and without the rule, 16 of 19 tests failed (e.g. "expected: <359> but was: <0>", "expected: <[10, 20, 30, 35]> but was: <[]>", "bestiary + 358 families + 359 in [...]"); the 3 that passed guard existing mechanisms and failed against a mutation (x-keys on `name`, no cap minimum, the notice path check off). - [x] **T5.2 feat(bestiary): live session tracking** (M, deps T5.1b, T5.2a, T5.2b, T1.9, T3.0d). Merges the three sources per family: the newer value wins, and each value keeps its source and age. Counts live kills. Subscribes to the readers only while the toggle is ON.

  - Live counting rules:
    - The first value is the baseline. Deltas of 1–50 count. A jump above 50 or a decrease resyncs without counting. Approximate values are skipped.
    - Baselines reset on a world/server change and on disconnect (T1.9 events); these resets do not end the session. A profile switch (T3.0d) resets the baselines and also ends the session (T5.2c, REQ-BEST-07).
  - The widget's next-tier threshold wins over the bundled one. The mismatch is logged once per family per session.
  - Milestone state comes only from menu or chat (10 tiers per milestone, minus later tier-ups) and is never computed from bundled totals.
  - R11 fallback, only if the G1 capture shows no usable live count: no live counting, rate and ETA show "—", state "live counting unavailable". No mob-death estimate in v1.
  - Accept: AC-BEST-03, AC-BEST-04, AC-BEST-10, AC-BEST-09 (the sequence 12,449 → "12.4k" → 12,452 gives +3), AC-BEST-17 (fallback case only).

Live kills only from exact widget counts: baseline, +1 to +50 counted, a jump or a fall resyncs, an abbreviated count skipped, a family listed twice in one update counted by its first line. The widget's next threshold wins over the table's, also past the table's last tier (no false MAX, `toMax` never below "to next"), logged once per family per session (INFO). A shared name the island cannot place keeps the family it was seen as. The milestone comes from the menu's item or chat: reached → 10 tiers to the next, each later tier-up one fewer; a claim only adds a milestone not yet seen, its tiers then unknown. An unknown name keeps the server's values (AC-BEST-15's display half). `profile(account, name, id)`: another name, or another id for the same name, starts over; unknown on a disconnect is ignored. `BestiaryModule` (Kotlin) subscribes the tab reader (ON and on SkyBlock), the chat reader and the menu reader (`BestiaryMenu.matchesTitle`, T5.2b) only while ON (REQ-GS-16), logs an unreadable chat line by its shape only, resets the baselines and the chat parser on every location change (world, server, disconnect) and on a toggle, and wires the resolver's and the tracker's notes to the log. Its card "Bestiary Tracker" in Odds & Trackers came forward from T5.3 with the toggle only (default OFF, §12.H), since every module needs its card (`OptionCatalogTest`); T5.3 adds its options, "Edit position" and the HUD. The category's description no longer names the odds R42 removed. CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]`: the tracker. No Minecraft or Fabric API beyond the readers' own; the gametest uses Fabric's `ClientReceiveMessageEvents` and the server-side `openMenu`, as `ChatReaderGameTest` and `MenuReaderGameTest` do.

Checks written after their code failed against a mutation: the 50/51 limits (49), the `name:` key, and `BestiaryGameTest` with the chat subscription off ("the hidden tier-up block is read: null"). After: `BestiaryTrackerTest` 17 and `BestiaryRulesTest` 1 pass; `./gradlew build` 1327 unit tests; gametests HookStatusGameTest, OpenCloseGameTest, SettingsScreensGameTest, BestiaryGameTest (OFF reads nothing; a block another mod hides is read; a toggle forgets a half-read block; the menu's milestone item gives 351 and 2), BundledDataGameTest, ConfigScreenGameTest, ConfigMotionGameTest and UiReviewMatrixGameTest pass; the new category's screenshot `t2.4a-category-odds_trackers` checked. Fixed: the false MAX past the table's last tier and its missing log; unchanged widget lines taken for new values; a family listed twice counting both lines; a shared name splitting into two entries; a profile made again under the same name; the kills' own source and age; kills made while OFF counting after a toggle, and a half-read block surviving it; the card's own widget command (REQ-GS-18); the 50/51 limits, the module's wiring (menu, toggle) and the table-less path untested; SettingsScreensGameTest and ConfigMotionGameTest assuming six modules and Fishing as the last tab; CHANGELOG, PLAN and SPEC §13 for the card.   - Accept: AC-BEST-08.
  - Done (2026-10-19): `BestiarySelection` (Kotlin): `select(tracker, mode, pins, hideMaxed)` and `pin(pins, key)`. Auto-follow shows the family whose count rose last: the tracker numbers each widget update and keeps, per family, the update of its last rise and its size (`FamilyView.changeOrder`, `lastRise`); in one update the bigger rise wins, then the name, then the id, so neither the line order nor the order families were first seen matters. A rise over 50 (counting no kills) and a changed abbreviated count are changes; a fall is none. Before any rise the family updated last shows (a menu page, a stored value); after one, a menu page does not take over. Pinned: up to 5 in the pinned order, unseen table families as views without values (`familyOrUnseen`, `updatedAt` 0), unknown keys left out; a sixth pin is refused, a repeated one changes nothing. "Hide maxed" filters before the pick in both modes. `BestiaryConfig`: `selection` AUTO, `pins` (repaired on load: no null, blank or repeated pin, at most 5), `hideMaxed` ON (R59, decided after the review asked for the default). The card's options are T5.3's.

Checks written after their code failed against a mutation: the bigger-rise rule removed, the name and id rules removed (Goblin, seen first, then won the tie), the null filter, the five-pin cap, and only counted rises (1–50) as changes. After: `BestiarySelectionTest` 8 pass; `./gradlew build` 1361 unit tests; the smoke set and `BestiaryGameTest` pass.

### Phase 6 — SkyBlock XP Optimizer (addendum)

Ranks every unlocked, not-yet-done SkyBlock XP task by XP per effective hour:
- `effective_h = time_h + coin_cost / coins_per_hour`
- `efficiency = xp / effective_h`

Around that: a Locked section showing what unlocks each task, a greedy "plan to target level" view, per-profile progress from the menus you open, and a patch-update skill. Calculation: AD-13. Data schema: §10 and `docs/sbxp/`. Modules: sbxp-optimizer (REQ-SBXP-*) and sbxp-update-skill (REQ-SKILL-*), decided by D-16–D-29, R14 and R15.
- **Gate:** no T6.1 code is written before T6.0 (your schema re-approval, REQ-SBXP-58).
- **Captures:** every `/sblevels` and component-menu capture is made at the G1 smoke (D-17). Nothing is captured before then. If a menu is missing from the G1 captures, its fixtures stay UNVERIFIED (REQ-GS-14) and I ask for that menu again at G6.
- **Reuses:** T1.9 (on SkyBlock), T2.3c/T2.4a (UI kit), T2.7/T2.8 (HUD), T3.0g (chat/action bar), T3.0c (registry), T3.0k (layered values), T3.0d (profile store), T3.0e (menu reader), T1.4a (back-off helper), T5.1 (bestiary table), T5.2b (Bestiary parser).

    - an unverified rate or drop chance may rest on one wiki page you supplied; `verified: true` needs 2 independent origins or one in-game capture (a `menu_capture` source, REQ-SBXP-04), and wiki + Fandom count as one
    - a `removed` change status with evidence (REQ-SKILL-04)

    - Your approval message comes before the first T6.1 commit.

  - Req: REQ-SBXP-01, REQ-SBXP-02, REQ-SBXP-03, REQ-SBXP-05, REQ-SBXP-06, REQ-SBXP-11, REQ-SBXP-12, REQ-SBXP-13, REQ-SBXP-14, REQ-SBXP-15, REQ-SBXP-16, REQ-DATA-13
  - Effort lines:
    - throughput, drop with pity (E/P50/P90), and fixed minutes
    - `/day` counts as a wall-clock wait only
  - Coins: gross only (D-19), from coin, item and convertible-currency lines plus running cost per hour.
  - Effective hours have a 1-minute floor.
  - Zero time and zero coins puts the task in Free / waiting (D-29). Any missing value means "no estimate".
  - Accept: AC-SBXP-03, AC-SBXP-07, AC-SBXP-08, AC-SBXP-09, AC-SBXP-10, AC-SBXP-11, AC-SBXP-12 (classification), AC-SBXP-13 (classification); EC-SBXP-01, EC-SBXP-12, EC-SBXP-15.
- [ ] **T6.1b feat(sbxp): options, cost classes, currencies, stage lookup, confidence** (M, deps T6.1).
  - Req: REQ-SBXP-17, REQ-SBXP-19, REQ-SBXP-20, REQ-SBXP-21, REQ-SBXP-22
  - Options and classes (R14):
    - Picks the cheapest option at the current coins/h and stage, after "exclude coin-only" has removed the coin-only options.
    - The class is set per option. A task's class follows from its options and never from coins/h.
  - Gems are never converted, and gem-only options are never chosen.
  - Stage lookup goes selected stage → `all` → nearest. A tie goes to the lower stage and forces low confidence.
  - Task confidence = the lowest of its components.
  - The suggested stage comes from your SkyBlock level and the data's `stages` thresholds; `mid` while the level is unknown.
  - Accept: AC-SBXP-14, AC-SBXP-16, AC-SBXP-17, AC-SBXP-18 ([A]), AC-SBXP-19 (lookup + suggestion), AC-SBXP-46 (option choice); EC-SBXP-02.
- [ ] **T6.1c feat(sbxp): XP crediting + override precedence** (S, deps T6.1b). Pure logic; overrides are stored per profile in T6.5c.
  - Req: REQ-SBXP-18, REQ-SBXP-23
  - XP crediting:
    - "best member counts" groups
    - derived XP (bestiary milestones) added to the steps that produce it
    - piecewise capped bands
  - Precedence for time: task time > task rate > rate-id > researched rate.
  - Precedence for coins: task coin > item price > live > bundled price (on top of REQ-DATA-10).
  - An override is flagged "outdated" only when the value it overrides changed or its id was removed. Each override can be reset on its own.
  - Accept: AC-SBXP-15, AC-SBXP-20; EC-SBXP-23.

    - Picks the most efficient eligible row and applies it: marks it done, unlocks dependents, advances ladders. Then recomputes.

  - Totals: play time, coins, effective hours, wall-clock waits, and the tasks left out for missing estimates.
  - Edge cases: "already reached", "short by N XP", a target above the max (D-24), an unknown current level.

    1. Check the preconditions. Ask for the update post and patch notes, **pasted or saved to a file**; never fetch hypixel.net. Version not newer and no upstream change → "up to date". No notes → data-source diffs only, labelled "no patch notes supplied" (R15).
    2. Bump the NEU pin and run `sbxpData` + the T6.2d diff. Removed tasks are kept and marked; renames become aliases only after you confirm. `--prices` asks before the AH scan.
    3. For changed families with no API/NEU value, ask for a `/sblevels` capture; without one, `null` / `verified: false`.
    4. Re-check the affected rates (REQ-SBXP-45–47, D-28 i).
    5. Update the JSON (`dataVersion` +1) and the research log, run `dataIndex`, then validate (`validateData -PbaseRef=<the commit the data branch starts from>`, which is `main` only after 2.0.0 has merged), run the tests and `./gradlew build`. Any failure stops the run.
    6. Write the CHANGELOG entry: old → new value, with source.
    7. **Gate 1** before committing on `data/sbxp-<gameVersion>` (`-d<dataVersion>` added only if that branch exists). **Gate 2** before push + PR. Never `main`, never force-push or merge.
  - `--dry-run` runs every step in a temporary detached worktree outside the repo, then deletes it. The repository stays unchanged.

### Phase 7 — Repository & release

Ships 2.0.0 the same way v1.1.0 shipped after G1 (phase 1): PR → your approval → merge → your separate "ship" → tag → draft → checks → publish. Order: T7.1 → T7.1b → T7.2 → T7.2b → T7.2c → T7.3 → T7.4 → T7.4b.

  - Req: REQ-REL-01, REQ-REL-02, REQ-REL-03, REQ-XC-TOGGLE-02, REQ-XC-RULES-07, REQ-GLOW-15, REQ-CORPSE-09
  - The corpse-spot licence line (REQ-CORPSE-09) left this task when R22 dropped corpse spots and is back with R40: the README says the corpse-spot data file is MIT, not CC0.
  - Supported version "Minecraft 26.2 (Fabric)": Java 25, minimum Loader, Fabric API, Hypixel Mod API; 26.1.x unsupported, 1.0.1 was the last 26.1 build.
  - Feature table (name, one line, category, default per §12.H; Rare Drop Odds, with its Croesus "≈ 1 in N runs" line (R10 = b), was here; dropped, R42). Every user-facing command. One placeholder per visible feature, as a committed image or text marker, so nothing renders as a broken image.
  - Compliance (P1–P7 in plain words, the excluded behaviours, the EC-GS-04 limitation), the non-affiliation sentence, network access (GitHub hosts per REQ-UPD-20, plus `api.hypixel.net` only when opted in, each with its toggle), licensing (CC0; MIT data → THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES; Render Chest Apache-2.0; credits).
  - No upgrade note: R34 took it out of the README and R51 out of the release notes.

It reads the release JSON that `gh api` saved, so Gradle never holds a token. T7.4 runs every draft check as one command.

    2. Annotated tag `v2.0.0` on the merge commit. `./gradlew clean build` from that commit with a clean tree; the build writes the sidecar (T1.15).
    3. Push the tag. `gh release create v2.0.0 --verify-tag --draft --title "K8bas Skyblock Utility v2.0.0" --notes-file <extracted section>` with the jar + `.sha256`.
    4. Draft checks:
       - `verifyReleaseDraft` on the authenticated `gh api` JSON
       - tier D boot of the exact jar with a copy of your mod set: no new ERROR lines against the G6 baseline, and the HUD editor lists only shipped elements
       - both gists still return a parseable array (the mob gist keeps the four invisible mobs, R38)

       Fix only inside the draft (`--clobber` on drafts only, EC-REL-05), then rerun all checks.
    5. With your OK, enable immutable releases via `gh api` before publishing.
    6. Publish (draft → public, `--latest` explicit, pre-release false), then run `gh release verify v2.0.0`. A wrong flag is fixed without touching the assets (EC-REL-09).
